\alpha=-2-\vartheta
to obtain

\lVert\mathbb{X}^{h}\rVert_{\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}}\lesssim\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert h\rVert_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})}(1\vee\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert^{2}_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})}).

To show that
\mathbb{X}^{h}\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\kappa}_{T}
it suffices to construct a sequence of smooth approximations to
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{h}
, see e.g. the proof of Lemma
D.1
.
∎

Appendix E
Regular Solutions to the Stochastic Heat Equation

In this appendix we analyse the regularity of the mild solution
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\bm{\xi}^{\delta}]
, where
\delta>0
is the correlation length and
\bm{\xi}^{\delta}=\psi_{\delta}\ast\bm{\xi}
denotes the mollification (in space) of a vector-valued space-time white noise
\bm{\xi}
against a mollifier
\psi_{\delta}
given by (
1.14
). Our goal is to establish that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, if the initial data of
\rho_{\textnormal{det}}
is assumed to be continuous, positive and of unit mass (Proposition
E.4
).

To show Proposition
E.4
, we first need to prove generic bounds on
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
that depend on
\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}
only through its existence and regularity (cf. Lemmas
E.1
,
E.2
and
E.3
). Therefore, to keep the notation concise, we denote
\sigma=\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}
and treat
\sigma
as a generic function of a given regularity until further notice.

Let
\gamma\geq 0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma)
,
\kappa\in[0,1/2)
and
\eta\in[0,1/2)
be such that
2\eta<\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
and
2\eta<1-2\kappa
. Below we establish
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
if
\sigma\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
(Lemma
E.1
),
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
if
\sigma\in L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
(Lemma
E.2
) and
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in L_{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
if
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{(\gamma^{\prime}\vee 0)}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
(Lemma
E.3
), each almost surely. We then use all of these results to establish Proposition
E.4
.

The Fourier transform of a vector-valued space-time white noise is given by a family complex-valued Brownian motions
W^{j}(t,m)\coloneq\bm{\xi}(\mathds{1}_{[0,t]}(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle}\otimes\delta_{j,\,\cdot\,})
, where
t\geq 0
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
j=1,2
(see Lemma
B.4
). Therefore, we can represent the Fourier transform of
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
by

\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}H^{j}_{t-u}(\omega)\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\varphi(\delta m)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(u,m),\qquad t\in[0,T],\quad\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}

(E.1)

see also
[
MM25
, (2.4)]
.
Hence taking the Fourier inverse, we can deduce the real-space representation

\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}(t,x)=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(u,m),\qquad x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}.

(E.2)

Recall from (
1.13
) that
\varphi
is assumed to be of compact support. Hence, the number of Brownian drivers contributing to (
E.2
) is finite and tends to infinity as
\delta\to 0
.

The following lemma establishes the regularity of
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
in an interpolation space (see Subsection
1.3
).

Lemma E.1
.

Let
T>0
,
\gamma\geq 0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma)
,
\kappa\in[0,1/2)
and
\eta\in[0,1/2)
be such that
2\eta<\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
and
2\eta<1-2\kappa
. Then for all
\sigma\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
\delta>0
and
p\in[1,\infty)
it holds that

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p,T}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}

(E.3)

and
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely.

Proof.

Using the real-space representation (
E.2
), we first establish for
\gamma\geq 0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma)
and
\eta\in[0,1/2)
satisfying
2\eta\leq\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
, that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}]^{1/2}\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}

(E.4)

and in particular
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely for each
t\in[0,T]
.

Let
(e_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Using the definition of
\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and Parseval’s identity, we obtain for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}&=\lVert(1-\Delta)^{\gamma^{\prime}/2}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Bigl\lvert\Bigl\langle e_{k},\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)\int_{0}^{t}(1-\Delta)^{\gamma^{\prime}/2}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(u,m)\Bigr\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert^{2}.\end{split}

An application of the stochastic Fubini theorem
[
Ver12
]
combined with Itô’s isometry yields

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}]&=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\langle e_{k},(1-\Delta)^{\gamma^{\prime}/2}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rangle_{L^{2}}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert(1-\Delta)^{\gamma^{\prime}/2}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu.\end{split}

(E.5)

We now distinguish the cases
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma-1)
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in[\gamma-1,\gamma)
. First let us assume that
\gamma^{\prime}\in[\gamma-1,\gamma)
. We can bound the integrand by an application of Schauder’s estimate
[
MM25
, Lem. A.5]
,

\begin{split}\lVert(1-\Delta)^{\gamma^{\prime}/2}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{L^{2}}&\lesssim\lVert P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}+1}}\\
&\lesssim(1\vee\lvert t-u\rvert^{-\frac{1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma}{2}})\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}},\end{split}

where we used that
\gamma\leq\gamma^{\prime}+1
. We bound by a change of variables and Jensen’s inequality uniformly in
u\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}&=\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\\
&=\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi(\omega+m)\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega)\rvert^{2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega)\rvert^{2}\\
&\lesssim\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}+(1+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim(1+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2},\end{split}

(E.6)

where the second inequality follows for
\gamma\in[0,1]
by the sub-additivity of
x\mapsto x^{\gamma}

and for
\gamma>1
by another application of Jensen’s inequality.

Consequently if
\gamma^{\prime}\in[\gamma-1,\gamma)
we may estimate uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}]&\lesssim\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}(1+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}(1\vee\lvert t-u\rvert^{-\frac{1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma}{2}})^{2}(1\wedge u)^{-2\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2},\end{split}

(E.7)

where we used
\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma
and
2\eta<1
to integrate the singularity;
2\eta\leq\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
to obtain a bound that is uniform in
t\in[0,T]
;
and
\textnormal{supp}(\varphi)\subset B(0,1)
to estimate the sum over
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.

For
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma-1)
, we estimate instead

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma^{\prime}+2)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}+1}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2},

where in the first inequality we used that
\gamma^{\prime}\in[\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}+1)
and (
E.7
) with
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}+1\geq 0
.

To summarise, it follows for all
\gamma\geq 0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma)
and
\eta\in[0,1/2)
satisfying
2\eta\leq\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
, that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

which yields (
E.4
) and that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Next by an application of Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion we establish (
E.3
) and the parabolic regularity
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
for arbitrary
\gamma\geq 0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma)
,
\kappa\in[0,1/2)
and
\eta\in[0,1/2)
satisfying
2\eta<\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
and
2\eta<1-2\kappa
.

Let
\kappa^{\prime}\in(\kappa,1/2]
be such that
2\eta\leq 1-2\kappa^{\prime}
.
Let
s<t\in[0,T]
; we distinguish the cases
\lvert t-s\rvert\leq 1
and
\lvert t-s\rvert>1
and first assume
\lvert t-s\rvert\leq 1
. Using the representation

\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}=P_{t-s}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(u,m)

combined with the fact that increments of
(W^{j}(\,\cdot\,,m))_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},j\in\{1,2\}}
over
[s,t]
are independent of
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]&=\mathbb{E}[\lVert P_{t-s}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]\\
&\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\lVert\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu.\end{split}

(E.8)

We bound the first summand in (
E.8
) by Schauder’s estimate
[
MM25
, Lem. A.5]
,

\lVert P_{t-s}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa^{\prime}}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}},

where we used that
\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}\leq\gamma^{\prime}\leq\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}+2
. Therefore by (
E.4
), we obtain uniformly in
s<t\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert P_{t-s}\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim_{T}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})^{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

(E.9)

To control the second summand in (
E.8
), we further distinguish the cases
2\kappa^{\prime}\leq 1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma
and
2\kappa^{\prime}>1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma
. Assume
2\kappa^{\prime}\leq 1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma
, we estimate the integrand as in (
E.4
) and obtain the singularity

\begin{split}&\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\lVert\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&\lesssim(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\int_{s}^{t}(1\vee\lvert t-u\rvert^{-\frac{1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma-2\kappa^{\prime}}{2}})^{2}(1\wedge u)^{-2\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu.\end{split}

Using that
\lvert t-s\rvert\leq 1
, we may bound

\int_{s}^{t}(1\vee\lvert t-u\rvert^{-\frac{1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma-2\kappa^{\prime}}{2}})^{2}(1\wedge u)^{-2\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu=\int_{s}^{t}\lvert t-u\rvert^{-(1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma-2\kappa^{\prime})}(1\wedge u)^{-2\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim_{T}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}},

where the singularity is integrable by
\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma
and
2\eta<1
; and the bound is uniform since
2\eta\leq\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
. Therefore if
2\kappa^{\prime}\leq 1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma
, we can control the second summand in (
E.8
) by

\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\lVert\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}.

Now assume
2\kappa^{\prime}>1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma
; we may skip the application of Schauder’s estimate, that is,

\lVert P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\lVert P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}\lesssim\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}},

to control the second summand in (
E.8
) by the singularity

\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\lVert\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\int_{s}^{t}(1\wedge u)^{-2\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu.

We may bound

\int_{s}^{t}(1\wedge u)^{-2\eta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim_{T}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}},

where the singularity is integrable by
2\eta<1
; and the bound is uniform by
2\eta\leq 1-2\kappa^{\prime}
. Therefore if
2\kappa^{\prime}>1+\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma
, we can control the second summand in (
E.8
) by

\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\lVert\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}.

To summarize, for all
\kappa^{\prime}\in(\kappa,1/2]
such that
2\eta\leq 1-2\kappa^{\prime}
, we can bound the second summand in (
E.8
) by

\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\lVert\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}.

(E.10)

Hence by (
E.8
), (
E.9
) and (
E.10
), we obtain uniformly in
\lvert t-s\rvert\leq 1
the bound

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}.

For
\lvert t-s\rvert>1
, we instead estimate by (
E.4
),

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}},

which yields for all
s,t\in[0,T]
the bound

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}]\lesssim_{T}(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\lvert t-s\rvert^{2\kappa^{\prime}}.

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, to control the
L^{p}(\mathbb{P})
-norm, we use Nelson’s estimate
[
FV07
, Lem. 2]
and Hölder’s inequality to deduce

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}]^{1/2},

which, together with the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-bound above, yields

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}-\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{s}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p,T}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}\lvert t-s\rvert^{\kappa^{\prime}}.

An application of Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion
[
FV10
, Thm. A.10]
then implies that there exists a modification (which we do not relabel) such that for all
p>1/\kappa^{\prime}
and
\kappa\in[0,\kappa^{\prime}-1/p)
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert^{p}_{C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa^{\prime}}}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p,T}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}.

(E.11)

Another application of Hölder’s inequality shows that this bound continuous to hold for every
\kappa\in[0,\kappa^{\prime})
and
p\in[1,\infty)
.

The next step is to generalize the result to arbitrary exponents
\kappa\in[0,1/2)
and to avoid the loss of
2(\kappa^{\prime}-\kappa)
space regularity in (
E.11
). Let
\gamma\geq 0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in[-1,\gamma)
,
\kappa\in[0,1/2)
and
\eta\in[0,1/2)
be such that
2\eta<\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
and
2\eta<1-2\kappa
. We can find
\kappa^{\prime}\in(\kappa,1/2]
such that
\gamma^{\prime}+2(\kappa^{\prime}-\kappa)\in[-1,\gamma)
,
2\eta\leq\gamma-(\gamma^{\prime}+2(\kappa^{\prime}-\kappa))
and
2\eta\leq 1-2\kappa^{\prime}
. An application of (
E.11
) with these inflated exponents yields

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert^{p}_{C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}-2\kappa}}]^{1/p}=\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert^{p}_{C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}+2(\kappa^{\prime}-\kappa)-2\kappa^{\prime}}}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p,T}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}},

which proves (
E.3
). Further, an application of Lemma
A.11
shows
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}_{T}^{\kappa}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely.
∎

The next lemma establishes the regularity of
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
as a square-integrable function in time.

Lemma E.2
.

Let
T>0
,
\gamma\geq 0
and
\sigma\in L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Then for all
\delta>0
and
p\in[1,\infty)
it holds that

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-1})\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}.

and in particular
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely.

Proof.

We apply Itô’s isometry (
E.5
), Parseval’s theorem and Tonelli’s theorem to obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}]&=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert(1-\Delta)^{\gamma/2}\partial_{j}P_{t-u}(\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\langle m,\,\cdot\,\rangle})\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2\lvert t-u\rvert\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\int_{u}^{T}\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2\lvert t-u\rvert\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}(1-\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-2\lvert T-u\rvert\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu,\end{split}

which we can bound by

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}]\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu.

Using that
\gamma\geq 0
, we bound the integrand as in (
E.6
) uniformly in
u\in[0,T]
,

\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\lesssim(1+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2},

which yields

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}]\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}(1+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sigma(u,\,\cdot\,)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\lesssim(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+1)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2},

where in the last inequality we applied
[
MM25
, Lem. C.2, (C.1)]
to control the sum over
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. The claim for arbitrary
p\in[1,\infty)
then follows by Nelson’s estimate
[
FV07
, Lem. 2]
and Hölder’s inequality.
∎

The next lemma establishes a uniform bound on
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
without trading space regularity for time regularity (cf. Lemma
E.1
).

Lemma E.3
.

Let
T>0
,
\gamma\in[-1,\infty)
and
\sigma\in C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma\vee 0}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Then for all
\delta>0
and
p\in[1,\infty)
it holds that

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L_{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-2})\max\{\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma\vee 0}},\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}\}.

Proof.

For all
\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
we obtain by (
E.1
) the stochastic differential equation of the Fourier transform
(\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega))_{t\in[0,T]}
,

\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)=-\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega^{j}\widehat{\sigma}(t,\omega-m)\varphi(\delta m)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(t,m),

whose (complex) martingale part we denote by

\widehat{M^{\delta}}(t,\omega)\coloneq\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}2\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 0.7mu\omega^{j}\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\varphi(\delta m)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muW^{j}(u,m).

The (sesquilinear) covariation of
\widehat{M^{\delta}}(t,\omega)
is given by

\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu[\widehat{M^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega),\widehat{M^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega^{\prime})]_{t}=\langle 2\pi\omega,2\pi\omega^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\widehat{\sigma}(t,\omega-m)\overline{\widehat{\sigma}(t,\omega^{\prime}-m)}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut

and we denote the complex quadratic variation by
[\,\cdot\,]_{t}\coloneq[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]_{t}
. It then follows by an application of the complex Itô formula, that

\begin{split}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\lvert\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)\rvert^{2}&=\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,-\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)+\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,-\omega)+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu[\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega)]_{t}\\
&=-2\lvert\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)\rvert^{2}\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mut+\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,-\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{M^{\delta}}(t,\omega)+\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(t,\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{M^{\delta}}(t,-\omega)+\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu[\widehat{M^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega)]_{t},\end{split}

which, combined with (
1.10
), yields

\begin{split}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{t}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}&=-2\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}_{u}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu+2\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(u,-\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{M^{\delta}}(u,\omega)\\
&\quad+\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}[\widehat{M^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega)]_{t}.\end{split}

(E.12)

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
, taking the supremum of (
E.12
) we can deduce the uniform bound

\begin{split}\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L_{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{p}&\leq 2^{p/2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl\lvert\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(u,-\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{M^{\delta}}(u,\omega)\Bigr\rvert^{p/2}\\
&\quad+\Bigl(\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}[\widehat{M^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega)]_{T}\Bigr)^{p/2}.\end{split}

(E.13)

To control the first term in (
E.13
), we use the (complex) Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
FV10
, Thm. 14.6]
, several applications of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
E.6
) to deduce for every
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl\lvert\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(u,-\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{M^{\delta}}(u,\omega)\Bigr\rvert^{p/2}\Bigr]\\
&\lesssim_{p}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl[\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{\,\cdot\,}\widehat{\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}}(u,-\omega)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5mu\widehat{M^{\delta}}(u,\omega)\Bigr]_{T}^{p/4}\Bigr]\\
&\leq\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma\vee 0}}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{p/2}](1+\delta^{-1})^{((\gamma\vee 0)+1)p/2}.\end{split}

(E.14)

To control the second term in (
E.13
), we estimate by (
E.6
) using that
\gamma+1\geq 0
,

\begin{split}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}[\widehat{M^{\delta}}(\,\cdot\,,\omega)]_{T}&=\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{\omega\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(1+\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2})^{\gamma}\lvert 2\pi\omega\rvert^{2}\lvert\widehat{\sigma}(u,\omega-m)\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(\delta m)^{2}(1+\lvert 2\pi m\rvert^{2})^{\gamma+1}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sigma_{u}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.5muu\\
&=(1+\delta^{-1})^{2(\gamma+2)}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.15)

Therefore it follows by (
E.13
), (
E.14
), (
E.15
) and Lemma
E.2
that for every
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L_{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{p}]&\lesssim_{p}(1+\delta^{-1})^{((\gamma\vee 0)+1)p/2}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma\vee 0}}^{p/2}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{p/2}]+(1+\delta^{-1})^{(\gamma+2)p}\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{p}\\
&\lesssim(1+\delta^{-1})^{(\gamma+2)p}\max\{\lVert\sigma\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma\vee 0}},\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}\}^{p}.\end{split}

To generalize the bound to
p\in[1,\infty)
, it suffices to apply Hölder’s inequality, which yields the claim.
∎

In the next proposition we specialize
\sigma=\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}
, where
\rho_{\textnormal{det}}
is a solution to the deterministic Keller–Segel equation (
2.1
) with initial data
\rho_{0}
and chemotactic sensitivity
\chi\in\mathbb{R}
(see Lemma
2.4
). A combination of Lemmas
E.1
,
E.2
and
E.3
with results of Section
2
yields the existence and regularity of
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}=\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\bm{\xi}^{\delta}]
in
C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
if
\rho_{0}
is assumed to be continuous, positive and of unit mass.

Proposition E.4
.

Let
\rho_{0}\in C(\mathbb{T}^{2})
be such that
\rho_{0}>0
and
\overline{\rho_{0}}=1
,
\rho_{\textnormal{det}}
be the weak solution to (
2.1
) with initial data
\rho_{0}
and chemotactic sensitivity
\chi\in\mathbb{R}
, and
T^{*}
be its maximal time of existence (see Lemma
2.4
). Then for all
T<T^{*}
and
\delta>0
it holds that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\coloneq\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\bm{\xi}^{\delta}]\in C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely and we can bound for every
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\gamma\in[-1,0]
,

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}\cap L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{p}\Bigr]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-2})\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{2}\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

(E.16)

Proof.

Let
\rho_{0}\in C(\mathbb{T}^{2})
be such that
\rho_{0}>0
and
\overline{\rho_{0}}=1
. An application of Lemma
2.6
yields
\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\in L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, which combined with Lemma
E.2
allows us to control for every
\gamma\in[-1,0]
,

\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim_{p}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-2})\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}\lesssim(1+\delta^{-\gamma-2})\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{1}}

(E.17)

and deduce
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in L^{2}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely.

To establish
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely, we use Lemma
E.3
and an approximation argument. By the density of the smooth functions in
C(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we can find a sequence
(\rho_{0}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\rho_{0}^{n}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
\rho_{0}^{n}>0
,
\overline{\rho_{0}^{n}}=1
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\rho_{0}^{n}\to\rho_{0}
in
C(\mathbb{T}^{2})
as
n\to\infty
.
Denote by
(\rho_{\textnormal{det}}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
the corresponding sequence of solutions to (
2.1
) with initial data
(\rho_{0}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and chemotactic sensitivity
\chi
. It follows by (the proof of) Theorem
2.2
that
\rho_{\textnormal{det}}^{n}\to\rho_{\textnormal{det}}
in
C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})

and an application of
[
BCD11
, Cor. 2.91]
combined with a cut-off argument as in Lemma
A.5
yields
\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}^{n}}\to\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\in C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.
Using that
\rho_{0}^{n}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\subset\mathcal{H}^{\vartheta}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
for every
\vartheta>0
, we can apply Lemma
2.6
to deduce
\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}^{n}}\in C_{\eta;T}\mathcal{H}^{\vartheta+2\eta}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
for each
\eta\in(0,1/2)
. Hence, Lemma
E.1
yields
\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}^{n}}\bm{\xi}^{\delta}]\in C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely (where we use that
2\eta<\gamma+2\eta
) and Lemma
E.3
yields
\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}^{n}}\bm{\xi}^{\delta}]\to\nabla\cdot\mathcal{I}[\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\bm{\xi}^{\delta}]=\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}
in
L_{T}^{\infty}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
as
n\to\infty
almost surely along a subsequence (which we do not relabel). It follows that
\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\in C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
almost surely by the completeness of
C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
under the uniform norm.

Finally, another application of Lemma
E.3
yields

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}}^{p}]^{1/p}&\lesssim_{p}(1+\delta^{-\gamma-2})\max\{\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{2}},\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+1}}\}\\
&\lesssim(1+\delta^{-\gamma-2})\max\{\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{2}},\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}}\}\end{split}

which combined with (
E.17
) proves (
E.16
).
∎

We apply the following infinite-dimensional sub-Gaussian upper deviation inequality in the proof of Theorem
3.6
.

Lemma E.5
.

Let
\rho_{0}\in C(\mathbb{T}^{2})
be such that
\rho_{0}>0
and
\overline{\rho_{0}}=1
,
\rho_{\textnormal{det}}
be the weak solution to (
2.1
) with initial data
\rho_{0}
and chemotactic sensitivity
\chi\in\mathbb{R}
, and
T^{*}
be its maximal time of existence (see Lemma
2.4
). Then for all
T<T^{*}
,
\delta>0
and
\lambda\geq 0
it holds that

(1+\delta^{-2})^{2}\log\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{2}\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{2}\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}}]\geq\lambda\Bigr)\lesssim-\lambda^{2}\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{C_{T}L^{2}\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{-2}.

Proof.

Denote
E\coloneq C_{T}L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\cap L_{T}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. An application of
[
Led96
, (4.4)]
yields for every
\lambda\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{E}-\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{E}]\geq\lambda\Bigr)\leq\mathrm{e}\mkern 0.7mu^{-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\quad\text{where}\quad\sigma\coloneq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in E^{\prime}\\
\lVert\xi\rVert_{E^{\prime}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[\langle\xi,\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

and Proposition
E.4
shows the existence of a constant
C>0
such that

\sigma\leq\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{E}^{2}]^{1/2}\leq C(1+\delta^{-2})\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{E}.

Both combined allow us to estimate

\log\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{E}-\mathbb{E}[\lVert\mathchoice{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}{\scalebox{0.64}{\,\includegraphics[page={32},width=2.84544pt]{diagrams_coloured.pdf}\,}}^{\delta}\rVert_{E}]\geq\lambda\Bigr)\leq-\frac{1}{2}C^{-2}\lambda^{2}(1+\delta^{-2})^{-2}\lVert\sqrt{\rho_{\textnormal{det}}}\rVert_{E}^{-2},

which yields the claim.
∎
